- EV-20260930-NOTIFICATION-AND-PANEL-ARCHITECTURE | Al Mousa notification appliance architecture + KGS identity + N16 persona/licence closure | test + runtime + drawing + manufacturer evidence | (1) NOTIFICATION ARCHITECTURE: the BOQ/legend "loop powered strobe(s)" label is NOT the device topology. The only Honeywell/NOTIFIER addressable loop-powered AV range (FS-WSO/BSO/WST/WSS/BSS) is rated "STROBE FLASH INTENSITY N/A >1cd" against a mandatory field-selectable 15/30/60/75/110 cd duty, and has no outdoor variant for the 100 exterior units. All three families (324 / 14 / 100) are therefore CONVENTIONAL_NAC: SD (indoor strobe), SHD (indoor horn/strobe), SHDK (exterior NEMA 4X horn/strobe), System Sensor SpectrAlert Advance (DN-7087:C1, A05-0456). FS-WSS is retained in the catalogue as EVALUATED_AND_REJECTED. Their zero SLC address consumption is a CONSEQUENCE of being NAC devices, resolving the earlier hybrid. (2) KGS IS THE FIRE COMMAND CENTRE, i.e. the campus MFACP -- proven by four sheets. That is why KGS never appeared among the six building-FACP names. KGS also has its own building FACP (6 loops). Governed evidence refutes that: DNR/DNRW carry `base_required` = "No", `device_role` = "SLC_HOUSING", `address_consumption` = "0", `address_resource_class` = "NONE"; B501-IV `compatible_detector_families` = "IDP-Photo, IDP-Photo-T, IDP-Acclimate, IDP-Heat, IDP-Heat-ROR, IDP-Heat-HT" and contains NO duct family; and IDP-PHOTO-R-IV `detector_base_requirement` reads "Also listed for use inside DNR(W) duct smoke detector housings; **when used standalone**, mounts to B300-6 or B501 base" -- the base condition is explicitly conditional on STANDALONE use. New `scripts/lib/al-mousa-detector-base-census.mjs` derives the quantity by summing only matrix rows that opt in, so re-adding a duct line cannot silently reintroduce a base. Base = 1401 smoke + 26 heat + 31 combined = **1458**, derived not hard-coded. The brief's candidate figure 1,458 is therefore CONFIRMED and is now reproduced by derivation. **Both DG Station heat candidates share the base:** B501-IV's compatible list covers IDP-Heat-ROR **and** IDP-Heat-HT, so an unresolved heat IDENTITY never made the base quantity unresolved -- confirming the brief's question. **CORRECTION 2 -- DUCT HEAD (IDP-PHOTO-IV -> IDP-PHOTO-R-IV, quantity unchanged at 45).** The ceiling detector was wrong for a duct housing on four independent grounds: DNR/DNRW `detector_base_requirement` = "Detector head sold separately; **requires IDP-PHOTO-R (remote-test capable) sensor**"; IDP-PHOTO-R-IV is described "Intelligent Photoelectric Replacement Smoke Detector **remote test capable, for use with DNR (W) duct smoke detector**" with `device_type` "Photoelectric Smoke Sensor, Remote-Test Capable (Duct Application)"; the NOTIFIER basis already selected the parallel R-variant head FSP-951R-IV; and Spec 28 46 00 Rev 1 PART 2 PRODUCTS clause 4 (p14) requires duct testing "locally via magnetic switch **or remotely**", which the R-variant satisfies and the plain part does not evidence. Using the ceiling part ALSO wrongly implied a B501-IV per duct location, so the two defects compounded. Verdict: **IDP-PHOTO-R-IV REQUIRED** (not merely "another model appears in a datasheet"). **CORRECTION 3 -- DG STATION HEAT (2 units RESOLVED to IDP-HEAT-ROR-IV).** Governing rule applied: a manufacturer capability or optional high-temperature model does not create a project requirement. That was an EVIDENCE-RETRIEVAL gap, not a manufacturer-uncertainty gap. **LOCAL EVIDENCE EXHAUSTED FIRST (per SS9):** the governed library has only 11 thin attributes on IFP-2100HV (240 V, 2.8 A, 2100 capacity, 159/159 per loop, 1 native loop, Ethernet, red) and a single `operating_voltage` on RPS-1000HV; the only registered Farenhyt datasheet source is **hbt-fire-351605-C.pdf, which covers the IFP-75 SERIES, not the IFP-2100**; and `engineering_facts` for IFP-2100HV asserts only panel-FAMILY compatibility, carrying no current rating or sync fact. A whole-database sweep for IFP-2100 / Flexput / RPS-1000 found no capacity fact anywhere. **EXTERNAL RESEARCH THEN USED (SS9 permits it over silent guessing), citing the publisher's own document repository prod-edam.honeywell.com.** **IFP-2100 / IFP-2100HV -- Honeywell Farenhyt Doc 351602 Rev C (04-2022):** "Flexput Circuits: Terminal block provides connections for (eight Class B or four Class A) NACs or auxiliary power. Power-limited, supervised circuitry. **Maximum current per circuit: 3 A. Cannot exceed 9A total for all circuits.**" AC 240 VAC 2.8 A (HV). **PER_CIRCUIT_LIMIT = 3 A and PANEL_TOTAL_LIMIT = 9 A are now governed, DISTINCT facts.** THE POINT: 8 circuits x 3 A = 24 A is WRONG for this panel -- the datasheet imposes a hard 9 A combined ceiling, so believing the naive figure would overstate the panel ~2.7x and erase the auxiliary-power requirement entirely. Class A halves the usable CIRCUIT COUNT (8 -> 4) but does NOT raise the 9 A ceiling. **SYNC = SYSTEM_SENSOR_SYNC_BUILT_IN_SUPPORTED.** Same Doc 351602 feature list: "Selectable strobe synchronization for Amseco, System Sensor, Wheelock, and Gentex devices." Therefore **MDL3 = NOT REQUIRED BY DEFAULT**. It is explicitly NOT ruled out permanently: a dedicated sync device may still be added if the eventual circuit architecture requires one and manufacturer evidence supports that case. **RPS-1000HV -- Honeywell Farenhyt Doc 350070 Rev M (04-2022):** "Provides 6.0 amps output power. Uses Flexput I/O circuits, 3A each..."; "Total Accessory Load: 6A @ 24VDC... Notification: 3 amps per circuit (6A system total)"; six on-board Flexput circuits; two Form C relays; compatibility list explicitly includes **IFP-2100HV** (63 max per panel); the HV suffix is the 240 VAC variant matching the IFP-2100HV supply. **AGGREGATE ARITHMETIC (design worst case 104.244 A unchanged):** per-panel usable 9 A x 7 panels = **base IFP capacity 63 A**; deficit = 104.244 - 63 = **41.244 A**; RPS-1000HV usable 6 A; **AGGREGATE_THEORETICAL_MINIMUM = ceil(41.244 / 6) = 7 x RPS-1000HV**. **THE 7 IS EXPLICITLY NOT A FINAL QUANTITY.** `FINAL_QUANTITY = PENDING_BUILDING_NAC_ALLOCATION`: the load is split across 7 physically separate buildings and spare Flexput output on one panel cannot serve another, so the per-building deficit must be computed and rounded UP per building. A per-building distribution can only INCREASE the installed count above the aggregate floor, never reduce it -- a campus with one heavily loaded building needs MORE units than 7, not fewer. This mirrors the SLC loop lesson already on record (aggregate minimum 10 vs installed 11). **CURRENT STATUS PRESERVED:** notification currents remain DESIGN_WORST_CASE for the two unresolved indoor groups and CONFIGURED at the spec-fixed 75 cd for the exterior group -- capacity evidence did NOT convert them into a final configured load. **HUMAN-INPUT REDUCTION:** IFP-2100HV NAC capacity, System Sensor sync support and "is MDL3 automatically required" are NO LONGER ENGINEER QUESTIONS -- all three are answered by manufacturer evidence. The ONLY remaining exact-P/N discriminators remain the three genuine configuration questions: wall vs ceiling mounting, body colour, and 2-wire vs 4-wire. **Candela is NOT a P/N blocker:** field-selectable output means the exact product CAN be selected while the per-room candela VALUE stays design-dependent, so PRODUCT P/N RESOLVED and FINAL CANDELA SETTING PENDING remain correctly separate states. | **VERIFIED** — 10/10 new suite `tests/al-mousa-nac-capacity-sync.test.mjs` (run with FA_DB); **776/777** across pricing engine + all Fire Alarm / golden Fire Alarm + Al Mousa + brand-strategy + task9 suites; `npm run test:identity` 27/27; **Fire Alarm golden GATE PASSED**; eslint **0 errors 0 warnings** on all 6 touched files; two consecutive repricing runs byte-identical. 4 stale assertions in `tests/al-mousa-notification-selection.test.mjs` (tests 5/7/9/12) encoded the PRE-verification world ("SYNC_HARDWARE_REQUIRED = PENDING", "NAC CAPACITY SUFFICIENCY = NOT COMPUTABLE", RPS `CONFIGURATION_REQUIRED`) and were updated to the verified world while KEEPING and in places STRENGTHENING the invariant: test 9 now asserts the superseded N16 70.0 A figure is ABSENT ENTIRELY rather than merely caveated, and test 3 of the new suite proves built-in sync does not equal "MDL3 can never be needed". Total standby AH"; **H "Alarm sounding period in hours. (For example, 5 minutes = 0.0833 hours)"**; I "Multiply lines E and H. Total alarm AH"; J "Add lines G and I."; then **"Multiply by the Derating Factor 1.25"**. **THREE CONSEQUENCES THAT CHANGE THE ANSWER:** (1) the alarm duration is entered in HOURS and the manual itself demonstrates converting minutes to hours, so the project's 30 minutes is a legitimate input as **0.5 h** following the identical arithmetic — and Tables 3.5/3.6, which tabulate only 5, 15 and 20 minute columns, are explicitly NOT the route for a 30-minute requirement, which resolves the prior slice's "20-minute table gap" concern in favour of the A-J worksheet; (2) the worksheet factor is an explicit **1.25**, which is the SAME 20% derating the manual says is "built in" to the Table 3.5/3.6 milliamp figures, so the two routes are ALTERNATIVE and the derating must not be applied twice; (3) **the RPS-1000 worksheets in 151153 Rev R end at line J and carry NO derating row**, so applying the panel's 1.25 to an RPS-1000 would add a factor that manual does not state — the model now applies no factor to RPS-1000 enclosures and records why. **A GENUINE AMBIGUITY IN THE WORKSHEET IS SURFACED RATHER THAN RESOLVED SILENTLY:** line D is a single total and both G and I derive from it, so the literal reading applies worst-case current to the STANDBY term as well. For a system carrying real notification load that returns **371.329 Ah** for one panel, far beyond anything the enclosure's own charger supports, which demonstrates it cannot be the intended selection route. The SLC device ledger is now included per panel using the conservative address pools (MFACP 420 det/129 mod, BOYS 476/123, GIRLS 481/120, WLC 151/55, SUB-1 5/5, SUB-2 4/4, DG 3/2) with detectors bounded on the -W/-IV figures and modules on IDP-MONITOR plus IDP-CONTROL SLC-and-auxiliary. Results: **largest panel requirement 29.629 Ah against the 17-55 Ah charger band, so the next listed size is 33 Ah; largest RPS requirement 3.839 Ah against the 7-33/35 Ah band, so the next listed size is 7 Ah**, which is also within the Table 2.3 24-hour standby limit of 270 mA (the computed standby is 40 mA) and within the 33 Ah FM cap. Both selections sit inside their bands, so **no enclosure needs a parallel bank — which project specification 1.10 M and both manufacturer manuals forbid in any case.** The selection follows the manual's own footnote 7 "Use next size battery with capacity greater than required" and its warning that undersizing "COULD OVERLOAD THE BATTERY RESULTING IN THE INSTALLATION HAVING LESS THAN THE REQUIRED 24 HOURS STANDBY POWER". **RPS-1000 CONFLICT NOW FIRST-PARTY CONFIRMED ON BOTH AXES AND STILL NON-MATERIAL:** installation manual 151153 Rev R Sec 3.5 states "The RPS-1000 battery charge capacity is 7 to 33 AH" and Sec 1.1 states "This cabinet is large enough to house two 17 AH batteries", while datasheet 350070 Rev M states "Battery charging capacity is 35AH" and "two 18AH backup batteries". Each pair is internally self-consistent, neither document acknowledges the other, and **no first-party erratum resolving it exists on prod-edam**. `RPS_BATTERY_CAPACITY_CONFLICT = NON_MATERIAL_TO_CURRENT_PROJECT` because the largest computed RPS requirement is 3.839 Ah, far below the LOWER bound; the conflict is RETAINED for future projects, not discarded. - EV-20261001-AL-MOUSA-BATTERY-CALCULATION-AUDIT | Blind independent audit of the Al Mousa battery result: the persisted 371 Ah "LITERAL_SINGLE_LINE_D" alternative is REPROVED FALSE and reclassified a PDF text-extraction artefact, and a SECOND and more serious error is found and corrected -- the previous result divided ampere-hours by 24 V, understating every battery by 24x and selecting an UNDERSIZED 17 Ah against a true ~30.2 Ah requirement | test + live read-only run + direct visual inspection of the manufacturer table (STRICTLY READ-ONLY; zero governed writes, no pricing, no quotation, no export, no commit/push/deploy) | Audit lane. **AUDIT VERDICT: THE PRIOR BATTERY RESULT WAS WRONG AND UNDERSIZED; the audited result replaces it.** Two independent defects were found, both in the immediately preceding slice's own output. **DEFECT 1 -- `LITERAL_SINGLE_LINE_D` = `PDF_TEXT_EXTRACTION_ARTEFACT`.** The prior slice retained an alternative that applied ONE worst-case total current to BOTH the standby and the alarm term, producing 371.329 Ah for one panel. The user of this audit asserted the worksheet has separate Standby Current and Alarm Current columns and asked for direct verification. **Verified by rendering page 24 of LS10143-001SK-E Rev E to an image and reading the table visually**, as the audit required rather than through flattened text. The rendered table shows the column header row `Device | # of Devices | Current per Device | Standby Current | Alarm Current`, and -- decisively -- **line D "Total current ratings of all devices in system (line A + line B + C)" is printed with TWO value cells, "mA" under Standby Current and "mA" under Alarm Current, and line E "Total current ratings converted to amperes (line D x 0.001):" is printed with TWO ampere cells.** Line G's result cell sits under the STANDBY column and line I's under the ALARM column. Flattened text extraction collapses this into a single "A A" token run, which is exactly how the artefact was created. The correct method is therefore `RequiredAh = (StandbyCurrent_A x standbyHours + AlarmCurrent_A x alarmHours) x DeratingFactor`, the bogus alternative is reclassified `PDF_TEXT_EXTRACTION_ARTEFACT` with `engineeringValid: false`, and the field that emitted it has been REMOVED from the returned object so it cannot be quoted as a result. A regression test asserts the artefact is never emitted as a computed figure and that the two worksheet columns are consumed separately at their own durations. **DEFECT 2 -- A 24x UNDERSTATEMENT, AND AN UNDERSIZED BATTERY SELECTION.** The prior slice computed `Ah = A.h / 24 V` on the reasoning that battery capacity is ampere-hours divided by battery voltage. **The manufacturer's worksheet does not divide by voltage at all:** line J is "Add lines G and I." and the next row is labelled "Total ampere hours required", so A.h IS the ampere-hour rating for the 24 V bank. The prior figures were therefore 24x too small: the largest panel read 0.989 Ah and selected **17 Ah**, whereas the true requirement is `(0.797 A x 24 h) + (12.152 A x 0.5 h) = 25.204 A.h x 1.25 = 30.245 Ah` -> **33 Ah**. The prior selection was **13.2 Ah UNDER the actual requirement**, which the manufacturer explicitly warns against: "FARENHYT DOES NOT SUPPORT THE USE OF BATTERIES SMALLER THAN THOSE LISTED IN TABLES 3.5 AND 3.6. IF YOU USE A BATTERY TOO SMALL FOR THE INSTALLATION, THE SYSTEM COULD OVERLOAD THE BATTERY RESULTING IN THE INSTALLATION HAVING LESS THAN THE REQUIRED 24 HOURS STANDBY POWER." This is a compliance-relevant error, not a presentational one, and it was found only because the audit rebuilt the calculation from zero rather than reusing the prior total. **BATTERY LEDGER REBUILT FROM ZERO WITH EXPLICIT POWER-SOURCE OWNERSHIP.** Every load is now a named row with quantity, per-device standby and alarm current, totals, manufacturer source and bank: IFP-2100 panel electronics (230/415 mA), 6815 expanders (78/78 mA each), smoke / heat / duct / combined detectors, manual stations, monitor modules, control modules, interface-zone modules, and the NAC load actually sourced by that panel. The per-class device mix is taken from the governed geometry-aware drawing takeoff and scaled to the governed conservative address pool by LARGEST-REMAINDER distribution, because the pool is the installed count while the drawing carries the class split. **Two implementation defects were caught while building it:** a naive "last key takes the remainder" scaling produced a **NEGATIVE module count** on a panel whose interface-module class had zero drawing quantity, and the ledger originally returned null totals whenever any row was unevidenced, which erased the whole bank instead of degrading it. Both are fixed, and the ledger now always exposes a `standbyFloorMa`/`alarmFloorMa` that sums only evidenced rows so an unevidenced row degrades the result rather than erasing it. **POWER-SOURCE OWNERSHIP AUDIT IMPLEMENTED AND PASSING.** `buildPowerSourceLedger` and `auditPowerOwnership` reject `UNASSIGNED_POWER_LOAD`, `DOUBLE_POWER_ASSIGNMENT`, `POWER_LOAD_ASSIGNED_TO_OTHER_BANK` and the new `PHYSICAL_MOUNTING_MISTAKEN_FOR_POWER_OWNERSHIP`. The last of these exists because a row may declare `ownerByWiring` -- the supply the manufacturer wiring connects it to -- independently of the bank it is booked into, and a disagreement means the cabinet a device sits in has been mistaken for its power source. **The governing rule is recorded explicitly: `PHYSICAL_MOUNTING_LOCATION != ELECTRICAL_BATTERY_SOURCE_OWNERSHIP`,** with the worked example that a 6815 mounted inside an RPS-1000 cabinet is still an SBUS device powered from the PANEL's SBUS power, because the RPS-1000 itself receives its SBUS power FROM the FACP, so its 78 mA belongs to the panel bank. A test proves that declaring such a device in an RPS bank is rejected while declaring it in the panel bank with `physicalMountedIn: RPS_CABINET` is accepted. NAC ownership is split so the panel bank carries only its native 9 A and the RPS bank carries its own share, and a test proves that leaving the RPS share in the panel bank is rejected. **BATTERY SELECTION AUTHORITY NOW SEPARATES THREE THINGS** so a box that can physically hold a battery is never treated as proof the battery is valid: `CALCULATED_REQUIRED_AH` from the load, `SUPPORTED_BATTERY_SIZES` (17, 18, 24, 33, 35, 40, 55 Ah), and `BATTERY_ENCLOSURE_CAPACITY` (main cabinet two batteries up to 18 Ah). The charger range 17-55 Ah is the sizing constraint; the cabinet is not. Accessory enclosures RBB (up to 35 Ah) and AB-55 (up to 55 Ah) are recorded for the four panels whose selected 24/33 Ah size exceeds the main-cabinet battery. **`BB-26` is recorded as `NOT_FOUND_IN_INSPECTED_EVIDENCE`** and no sizing decision depends on it. **THRESHOLD SENSITIVITY NOW REPORTED PER PANEL** with required Ah, the next lower listed size, the margin to that lower size, the selected size, the headroom, and the NEXT step up, because a small current uncertainty is only material if it crosses one of those thresholds. **NO GOVERNED WRITE:** `slc_expansion_max_count` still `12`, `product_attributes` unchanged at 115 rows, BOQ merges still 8. **VERIFIED:** 12/13 of the new `tests/al-mousa-battery-audit.test.mjs` and **436/437** across all `tests/al-mousa-*.test.mjs` + `tests/farenhyt-*.test.mjs`; eslint 0 errors 0 warnings on five touched files; runner output byte-identical across three runs. **THREE PRIOR-SLICE TESTS BROKE ON THE CORRECTION AND WERE HANDLED EXPLICITLY, NOT WEAKENED:** one asserted that the now-disproved artefact figure was "more conservative" and was replaced with an assertion that it is `engineeringValid: false` and is not emitted at all; one asserted an unrounded `alarmAh` against a value the implementation rounds to 3 dp for deterministic reporting; and one banned the bare string `"FROM "` as a proxy for SQL, which my own prose ("SBUS power FROM the FACP") tripped -- it was narrowed to a real `SELECT ... FROM` pattern. Four further test-authoring errors of mine were corrected: two over-tight float tolerances, a wrong assertion that two equal input currents must yield equal ampere-hours (they cannot, because the durations differ by 48x), and a `\\.25` double-escaping slip. **THE ONE REMAINING FAILING TEST IS THE OPEN ITEM THIS AUDIT WAS ASKED TO CLOSE:** `IDP-PULL current must be first-party evidence, not zero` still fails because the manual-station current remains unevidenced, so the ledger correctly reports it as a floor and no Ah contribution is computed for it. A delegated read-only agent had reported this as a single "350uA"; **that was a misreading and is corrected.** The datasheet gives TWO separate values -- standby 375 uA and alarm 5 mA -- whereas the panel current-draw worksheet LS10143-001SK-E Rev E Table 3.2 (Continued), Addressable SLC Modules, gives a single combined **"IDP-PULL-SA/IDP-PULL-DA Standby/Alarm 0.3 mA"**. **The two first-party documents therefore state a genuinely different parameter, not merely a different number**, so the matter is recorded as `OFFICIAL_DOCUMENTATION_CONFLICT` with `conflictClass: HIGHER_VALUE_USED__BOTH_RETAINED`: the data sheet figure is higher in BOTH conditions (375 uA > 300 uA standby, 5 mA > 300 uA alarm) so it is used for sizing, and the worksheet figure is retained. Neither document labels the value typical, nominal, max or maximum, which is itself recorded; the governing basis is the worksheet's own preamble at 3.5.1, "For the IFP-2100, the worst case current draw is listed for the panel, addressable devices, and SLC expanders." `MANUAL_STATION_CURRENT_UNEVIDENCED` is removed, every panel bank is now complete rather than a floor, and the data sheet's compatibility list is recorded as naming IFP-2100 / IFP-2100ECS / RFP-2100 but NOT IFP-2100HV or IFP-2100ECSHV, so HV applicability rests on the panel manual's own worksheet. **A SECOND, MORE CONSEQUENTIAL CORRECTION: THE 6815 POWER-OWNERSHIP RULE WAS WRONG AND WAS MY OWN UNEVIDENCED ASSUMPTION.** The prior turn asserted that "a 6815 mounted inside an RPS-1000 cabinet is still an SBUS device powered from the PANEL's SBUS power", on the reasoning that the RPS receives its SBUS power from the FACP. **The first-party text refutes this.** RPS-1000 installation manual 151153 Rev R, re-read by the main agent, states verbatim: **"RPS-1000 Terminals 30-33 are used only for connection RPS-1000 to the FACP or to the controlling RPS-1000. Use RPS-1000 Terminals 16-19 to connect other SBUS modules (SLC expanders, annunciators, 5824) and to daisy-chain RPS-1000 modules."** Its terminal table rates terminals 18/19 "SBUS power 24 VDC 1.0 A" but terminals 32/33 "MAIN RPS-1000 SBUS power (from FACP) 24 VDC 10 mA", so an upstream feed cannot carry a 78 mA expander. Decisively, **the same "6815 SLC Loop Expander Standby: 78 mA / Alarm: 78 mA" row appears in BOTH the IFP-2100 worksheet (Table 3.2) and the RPS-1000 worksheet (Table 2.4)** -- the manufacturer's own accounting showing the expander is charged to whichever unit's SBUS OUT it is wired to. **The rule is therefore re-stated as `ELECTRICAL_TERMINAL_PAIR_DETERMINES_OWNERSHIP__NOT_PHYSICAL_CABINET`**, and the same physical board can be either bank's load depending on the terminal pair used. The new ledger check `PHYSICAL_MOUNTING_MISTAKEN_FOR_POWER_OWNERSHIP` compares a row's declared bank against its `ownerByWiring` and rejects a mismatch, and the test suite now proves that a panel-wired expander in an RPS cabinet is a PANEL load, an RPS-wired expander is an RPS load, and only a mismatch between the two is rejected. **THE UNPROVEN WIRING DECISION IS MATERIAL TO THE BATTERY SIZE.** The governed drawings show the campus panel network (fibre between each FACP and the MFACP) but contain NO 6815 SBUS wiring, so the wiring is NOT EVIDENCED. Both scenarios are therefore computed: **Scenario A, all expanders panel-wired, gives MFACP 31.604 Ah, BOYS 30.783 Ah, GIRLS 30.848 Ah, WELCOME CENTER 21.183 Ah, SUB-1 10.531, SUB-2 10.403, DG 10.153 -> 33/33/33/24/17/17/17 Ah. Scenario B, expanders moved onto an RPS-1000's own SBUS OUT, drops the three main panels to 20.1-19.4 Ah -> 24 Ah, a reduction of 11.466 Ah each, because 5 x 78 mA of 24-hour standby moves off the panel bank.** WELCOME CENTER and the three station panels are unchanged between scenarios because they have no RPS, so their expanders cannot move. The panel SBUS ampacity of 1.0 A bounds how many may move. Scenario A is carried as the conservative selection. **OTHER FACTS CLOSED IN THIS PASS:** SK-NIC is evidenced at 21 mA standby and 21 mA alarm (Doc 351622 Rev A) and is powered from the PANEL's own P7 Data Network pin, so it is owned by the panel bank; its applicability is recorded as CONDITIONAL because the IFP-2100 has an integral network port and the drawings do not state whether any panel carries an SK-NIC. The 5496 NAC expander is recorded as NOT USED but with its true character: it "provides its own AC power connection, battery charging circuit, and backup battery" (Doc 350302 Rev H), so only its 10 mA "(SBUS)" presence current lands on the panel. The 5495/5499 is recorded at 75/205 mA, NOT USED, with the negative result that no first-party 5495/5499 data sheet exists. **BB-26 IS CORRECTED FROM `NOT_FOUND` TO `EXISTS_BUT_NOT_AN_IFP_2100_ACCESSORY`:** it does exist as Honeywell/Silent Knight Doc. 51858 Rev A 09/14/2001, a passive dead-front backbox housing two 12 VDC 26 Ah batteries with an optional separate CHG-75 charger, but the string "BB-26" appears nowhere in the 178-page IFP-2100 manual and no BB-26 entry exists in the prod-edam datasheets (5,465 files) or installation-guides (2,366 files) listings, so it is a legacy Silent Knight / Fire-Lite accessory and is NOT an authorised IFP-2100 accessory. A test asserts it is never modelled as a capacity option. **BATTERY SELECTION AUTHORITY FULLY CONFIRMED:** supported sizes 17, 18, 24, 33, 35, 40, 55 Ah (Table 3.5) and 33, 40, 55 Ah (Table 3.6); the rule is footnote 7, "Use next size battery with capacity greater than required"; charger 17-55 Ah; the ONLY first-party in-cabinet limit is the datasheet's "Battery: Cabinet holds maximum of two 18 AH batteries", which the installation manual does NOT state anywhere in its 178 pages; That permits two opposite ungoverned failures: trivially easy items pay for the strongest model, and genuinely ambiguous items never get a stronger second look. **THE NEW MODULE `app/domain/ai-provider-escalation-policy.mjs` SUPPLIES THE MISSING DECISION AND ONLY THE DECISION.** It declares the closed ladder `ESCALATION_TIERS = [LIGHTNING, SUPER, ULTRA]` with per-tier `role` and `intendedFor`, and `decideEscalation` derives a tier purely from the ALREADY-GOVERNED response shape -- the BOQ understanding engine emits per-fact `origin` in EXTRACTED / INFERRED / MISSING and `confidence` 0-100, plus top-level `confidence` and `corrections`, so the module reads the real governed output instead of inventing a parallel signal. **IT ENFORCES FIVE INVARIANTS:** (1) NO HIDDEN FALLBACK -- a tier changes only by producing an explicit reason, so the caller cannot silently pick a model; (2) AI NEVER ESCALATES INTO AUTHORITY -- `humanAuthorityRequired` yields `HUMAN_REVIEW_REQUIRED`, which is TERMINAL, outranks every tier, selects NO model, and wins even over a cross-document conflict, so a bigger model can never convert a review into an approval; (3) THE LADDER IS CLOSED -- an unknown entry tier returns `INVALID_TIER` with `provenance: null` and is never a passthrough; (4) EVERY DECISION IS EXPLAINED -- `buildProvenance` records policy version, entry tier, decided tier, reasons, available tiers and a caller-supplied `observedAt`, and sets `aiAuthorityGranted: false` on every record; (5) DEGRADATION IS EXPLICIT -- if the target tier is unavailable the result stays at the entry tier with `degraded: true` and reason `HIGHER_TIER_UNAVAILABLE`, and exhausting the ladder yields `EXHAUSTED_LADDER` with `requiresHumanReview: true` rather than inventing a stronger tier or silently accepting. Cross-document conflict is the single signal that jumps straight to ULTRA, because several authoritative sources disagree and one re-read is unlikely to settle it. **THE MODULE IS PURE BY CONSTRUCTION AND PROVEN SO:** no network, no clock, no credential, no project or database access, and it deliberately does NOT import the provider -- test 12 asserts no import path matches `boq-understanding-provider|worker/` and that the source contains no `createConfiguredNvidiaNimStructuredProvider`, `apiKey` or `Bearer`, so the other lane's transport stays authoritative and this file cannot become a shadow provider. Determinism is proven by deep-equality of two identical calls and by a decision fingerprint that is a function of the DECISION, not the clock. **A KNOWN TEST ANTI-PATTERN RECURRED AND WAS FIXED CORRECTLY.** Test 11 banned the tokens `fetch(`, `Date.now`, `new Date`, `process.env`, `require(`, `await `, `nvidia`, `NVIDIA` and `chat/completions`, and failed on `NVIDIA` -- the banned list matched its OWN PROSE, because the module header legitimately NAMES the provider it refuses to import. The fix was NOT to delete the documentation and NOT to drop `NVIDIA` from the list: the assertion was narrowed to EXECUTABLE CODE by stripping comments first, with two self-checks proving the stripper did not gut the source, and `integrate.api.nvidia.com` was ADDED to the banned list. **the main agent then read the primary document itself** (9 pages, extracted with pymupdf) and confirmed four clauses verbatim. **§2.6(a) -- CONFIDENTIAL DATA IS PROHIBITED, NOT MERELY DISCOURAGED:** *"If you make available User Content or create Generated Content through NVIDIA API Catalog, you agree you will not: (a) include any confidential information, controlled or sensitive data, including protected health information, personal data (unless expressly permitted by an API Service), payment card industry information or sensitive human subject research, or data that was processed or collected in violation of law."* **§1.2 -- TRIAL ONLY:** *"NVIDIA will provide you access to the API Service for limited trial purposes only and without use of the API Service or Generated Content in production."* **§1.4 -- NOT PRODUCTION:** *"you may only use the API Service for internal testing and evaluation purposes, not in production"*, and a separate Subscription is required for production use. **§3.3(iv) -- INPUTS ARE RECORDED AND USED TO TRAIN NVIDIA PRODUCTS:** NVIDIA collects *"(iv) User Content and Generated Content to improve NVIDIA products and services, including AI models"*, and *"Your use of the API Services will be logged for security, fraud or abuse monitoring and shared with third party service providers for this purpose."* A narrow carve-out exists at §2.4 (30-day User Content / 90-day Generated Content retention) but it is scoped to the **Fine Tuning API Service** and to services *expressly disclosed* in the catalog; the §2.3 default is no end-of-session retention, which sits in tension with §3.3(iv), and the **trial framing governs**. **CONSEQUENCE FOR THIS PROJECT:** tender documents, supplier quotations, client documents, proprietary price lists and the Central Kitchen benchmark corpus are all confidential, so **none of them may be sent to the hosted catalog** -- not for diagnostics, not for shadow testing, not for a one-off. That is precisely a table-structure problem, and both candidates are hosted. **THE THREE CORRECTIONS TO THE BRIEF'S OWN PREMISES ARE CONFIRMED AND MATERIAL.** (1) *"Super"* and *"Ultra"* **do exist** as named hosted tiers -- the brief's suspicion was wrong in the safe direction. (2) `llama-3.2-nv-vision-embedqa-1b-v2` **does not exist** (HF 401, absent from the hosted index); the live VL names are the `nemotron-embed-vl` / `nemoretriever-vlm-embed` / `nvclip` family. (3) `nvidia-rerank-qa-mistral-4b` is the **wrong name**; the real one is `nvidia/nv-rerankqa-mistral-4b-v3`. **THE BUILD-SITE IS AN UNRELIABLE EXISTENCE ORACLE, AND THE MAIN AGENT PROVED IT WITH A NEGATIVE CONTROL.** `build.nvidia.com` returns **HTTP 200 for every path**, including the invented slug `totally-not-a-real-model-xyz`, so a 200-status existence check silently "verifies" nonexistent models. Two NVIDIA-owned broken links are recorded for upstream reporting: `build.nvidia.com/nvidia/nemotron-ocr` and `docs.nvidia.com/nemo/retriever/latest/extraction/overview.html`. **THE NVIDIA GPU ASSUMPTION IS WEAKER THAN THE BRIEF ASSUMED FOR TWO COMPONENTS, WHICH MATTERS ON APPLE M1.** NeMo Retriever is **not** GPU-mandatory: its README states that for workloads under ~100 PDFs it *"works with HuggingFace models on local GPUs **or with NIMs hosted on build.nvidia.com**"*, and the `nemo-retriever` install variant is remote-NIM and needs no GPU. `rag-blueprint` v2.6.0 can run its **models** from the hosted catalog, but the skill's own control plane is Docker/Compose or Kubernetes/Helm and its `allowed-tools` are `docker`, `kubectl`, `helm`, `nvidia-smi` -- so the blueprint needs **Docker**, which is the practical blocker, not CUDA. `rag-eval` v2.6.0 needs **no GPU** (it is a client) but does need a **running RAG deployment**, Python 3.11+ and `uv`, and an `NVIDIA_API_KEY` for its RAGAS **LLM judge** whose default judge model is `mistralai/mixtral-8x22b-instruct-v0.1`, a **third-party** OpenAI-style model rather than an NVIDIA-hosted one. Its three NVIDIA-specific metrics are **`nv_accuracy`**, **`nv_context_relevance`** and **`nv_response_groundedness`**, and it is tightly coupled to NVIDIA's own repo layout -- so the reusable asset is the **metric definitions**, not the skill. `data-designer` requires **no GPU** at all (Python >=3.10, a configured LLM backend, and network access to it) and is genuinely usable, but it generates synthetic **training/eval** data, not parsed documents. **THE PRIORITY HYPOTHESIS IN THE BRIEF IS REJECTED AS STATED, ON THE PROJECT'S OWN EVIDENCE.** The brief ranks Priority 1 = Retriever/Embeddings/Reranking and Priority 2 = Document Intelligence. For THIS project the evidence inverts that: the one place where an AI component demonstrably and catastrophically failed was **document/table structure**, and the one place where deterministic identity matching demonstrably works is **exact model-number resolution** (`IDP-PHOTO-IV`, `IDP-PULL`, `6815`, `RPS-1000`, all of which resolved correctly from first-party documents and manufacturer evidence). Its `sheet`/`row_number` columns are nullable and the existing catalogue already stores `sheet: null` for non-spreadsheet sources, so it expresses a document locator perfectly well via `page` + `original_text`. - EV-20261001-NVIDIA-SYNTHETIC-BENCHMARK-NATIVE-BASELINE-AND-ESCALATION-GATE | Synthetic-only benchmark pass: the native document parser is MEASURED at 6/15 on a 15-case authored corpus and REPRODUCES the merged-cell understatement class at 3x on fabricated data, AND the declared escalation policy is MEASURED to be NON-DISCRIMINATING (escalates 100% of cases, threshold sweep completely flat) and is therefore REFUSED for production wiring | test + real runs of the canonical parser + policy sensitivity analysis (STRICTLY READ-ONLY on project data; zero governed writes, no pricing, no quotation, no export, no commit/push/deploy, no Central Kitchen answer key opened, NO confidential data transmitted anywhere, no live NVIDIA call attempted) | Benchmark/calibration lane. **`NVIDIA_API_KEY` IS ABSENT, so per §11 EVERY LIVE BENCHMARK IS `LIVE_NVIDIA_BENCHMARK_BLOCKED_BY_CONFIGURATION` and NO NVIDIA RESULT WAS FABRICATED.** The probe reports the blocked status, and all 28 tests are mock- or blocked-path only. **THE HEADLINE EMPIRICAL RESULT IS A MEASURED NATIVE FAILURE, REPRODUCED WITH FABRICATED DATA.** A 15-case synthetic document-structure corpus was authored with exact, independently authored ground truth, and the REAL canonical parser (`boq-engine-1.0.2`, ruleset `construction-boq-2026-08-12.2`, method `native-openxml`) was run over genuine XLSX bytes containing genuine `<mergeCells>` markup. Case `SYN-DOC-001` reproduces the historical failure CLASS with entirely invented content: a BOQ whose current columns are merged across three device rows (`E2:F4`), holding a single 6.5 mA alarm current. **The parser correctly DETECTS the merge (`mergedRanges: ["E2:F4"]`) and correctly LABELS the columns (`labels: ["item","description","unit","qty","standbyma","alarmma"]`, header quality 98, confidence 99) -- and then does NOT propagate the merged value to the rows it covers.** Only 1 of 3 device rows recovers the current; rows 2 and 3 return null. Consequence: a per-row current total recovers **6.5 mA where 19.5 mA is correct -- a 3x understatement, expressed in the authored key as `understatementFactor: 3`, `understatementErrorMa: 13`.** This is the same structural class as the historical 24x battery error, and it is now reproducible on demand from fabricated data. **THE DIAGNOSIS MATTERS MORE THAN THE SYMPTOM, AND IT FAVOURS A NATIVE FIX:** the information is PRESENT in the parser's own output (`mergedRanges` is parsed and exposed) and is simply not applied, so this is a **column-mapping gap, not a limitation of OpenXML extraction**. **FULL NATIVE BASELINE, MEASURED: 6/15 passed, passRate 0.4667, 3 P0 structural failures and 5 P1 semantic failures.** Failing: `SYN-DOC-001` (P0, merge not propagated), `SYN-DOC-003` (P0, two-value row), `SYN-DOC-004` (P0, repeated blank description yields 0 items), `SYN-DOC-012` (P0, remarks text becomes quantity), `SYN-DOC-002`/`005` (P1, header depth), `SYN-DOC-006` (P1, units), `SYN-DOC-007` (P1, child item count), `SYN-DOC-014` (P1, section heading). Passing: `008`, `009`, `010`, `011`, `013`, `015`. **A FAIL-CLOSED BEHAVIOUR WORTH KEEPING WAS OBSERVED:** the parser raises `No BOQ table was found in the workbook.` on sheets whose header vocabulary it does not recognise, rather than guessing -- correct behaviour, and the harness records it as a reportable decline rather than a crash. **THE MOST IMPORTANT RESULT IS ACTUALLY NEGATIVE, AND IT REFUSES MY OWN PRIOR RECOMMENDATION: THE ESCALATION POLICY IS NON-DISCRIMINATING AND MUST NOT BE WIRED.** Running the current `decideEscalation` over the 36-case authored understanding corpus gives **`escalationRate = 1.0`, `acceptedAtLightning = 0`, 33 routed to SUPER and 3 to ULTRA, with the accept-confidence sweep COMPLETELY FLAT across 50/60/70/80/90/95 (36 escalations at every value).** The mechanism was isolated rather than guessed: the `acceptConfidence = 80` threshold **works correctly in isolation** (a fully-extracted response at confidence 90 is accepted, at 80 it escalates), but the `MISSING_EVIDENCE` rule fires on **a single missing field, independent of confidence**, and every well-formed BOQ understanding line legitimately has at least one field that must stay unknown (manufacturer, model). **So a model with excellent unknown-preservation discipline escalates 100% of the time, and the confidence threshold does no work at all.** Wiring this would spend Super and Ultra budget on every line for no discrimination. Per §18 ("do not wire unless the benchmark shows useful discrimination") the gate **FAILS**, and the previous pass's 🟢 ADOPT for the escalation policy is **downgraded**. The thresholds were deliberately **NOT** re-tuned: §17 says measure first, and tuning against no model output would be fitting noise. **A CALIBRATION HONESTY NOTE: the TP/FP/FN/TN that §17 asks for CANNOT be computed here** because it requires real Lightning errors, Super results and Ultra results, and there is no model. Those four numbers are reported as `BLOCKED__NO_LIVE_MODEL__NVIDIA_API_KEY_ABSENT` and were NOT estimated. What was measured instead is decision distribution, agreement with AUTHORED difficulty labels (0.6667) and with AUTHORED human-review labels (**1.0 -- authority termination is exact**), and threshold flatness. The output is explicitly labelled `POLICY_SENSITIVITY_NOT_A_MODEL_BENCHMARK`. **THREE REAL DEFECTS IN MY OWN BENCHMARK CODE WERE FOUND AND FIXED, ALL DISCLOSED BECAUSE EACH WOULD HAVE PRODUCED A FALSE RESULT.** (1) **The scorer coerced missing values to zero**: `Number(null)` is 0 and `Number("")` is 0, so an absent current was scored as a present zero, which made case 001 report `preservedIntendedRowRelationship: true` when the parser had in fact recovered only one of three rows. That is *precisely* the understatement class under test, reproduced inside the measuring instrument; `num()` now returns null for null/undefined/empty/boolean and test 13 pins it. (2) **An off-by-one attributed a device's current to its neighbour**: the BOQ row's sheet-row number lives at `r.source.row`, NOT at `r.sourceRow` (absent) and NOT at `r.sequence` (1-based over emitted rows, which diverges from the sheet row whenever a header or section row precedes data). Using `sequence` reported OP2 as carrying 6.5 mA when OP1 did. Caught by cross-checking the raw sheet against the mapped rows. (3) A first harness run reported 0/15 and a `parser_threw` P0 on every case, which was **my** wrong assumption about the parser's output shape (`quantity` is `{numeric}`, `unit` is `{original, normalized}`, provenance lives in `source.cells` with cell-level refs such as `D2`, and there is no `sheets` key), plus two temporal-dead-zone errors from statements placed inside an object literal. The lesson is recorded because it is the same class of error this pass exists to catch: **an audit that does not rebuild from the engine's real output will confidently report nonsense.** **A GENUINELY POSITIVE FINDING, MEASURED: THE NATIVE PARSER EMITS CELL-LEVEL PROVENANCE.** Each row carries `source: { kind, sheet, page, row, cells: { itemNumber: "A2", description: "B2", unit: "C2", quantity: "D2" } }`, so §29's provenance requirement is **already met by the native path** and would be a NVIDIA regression risk, not a gain. **DATA-DESIGNER EVALUATION IS BLOCKED AND MEASURED AS SUCH:** the `data-designer` CLI and Python module are both ABSENT and no LLM backend credential of any kind is configured, so §20-21 could not be executed and no synthetic corpus was machine-generated. **All 36 understanding cases and 15 document cases were HAND-AUTHORED instead, which is arguably better for a ground-truth benchmark: a generator's output cannot author its own answer key.** **PRIVACY GUARD BUILT AND PROVEN (§31), NARROW BY DESIGN:** `guardSyntheticInputPath` refuses 8 categories of known-private path -- `inputs/central-kitchen`, `final-quotation`, `central-kitchen`, `al-mousa`, `al_mousa`, `uploads`, `project-upload`, `supplier-price`, `supplier_price`, `price-list`, `pricelist`, `source-boq`, `outputs` -- and is **fail-closed**, so an unrecognised path such as `package.json` or `/etc/hosts` is REFUSED as `REFUSE_OUTSIDE_SYNTHETIC_ROOTS` rather than allowed by default. A private path nested INSIDE a permitted root is still refused with the matched fragment reported, path traversal out of a permitted root is refused, and `authoriseTransmission` reports EVERY refusal rather than the first. It inspects paths only: it does not walk directories, read files, or act as a global filesystem blocker. Test 28 additionally proves that **no benchmark module contains a `fetch(` call or a hardcoded hosted endpoint**, and that the probe itself contains neither a `fetch(` nor an `Authorization`/`Bearer` string. **DATA-ISOLATION PROVEN MECHANICALLY, NOT BY PROMISE:** test 4 rejects any part-number-shaped token that is not `SYN-*`/known-benign, so no real manufacturer part number can enter a fixture; test 3 rejects any vendor model vocabulary (`nemotron`, `nv-`, `nvidia`) appearing in a ground-truth block, so NVIDIA can never be scored against a key it helped write; test 7 rejects project UUIDs, the project name, and absolute `/Users|/Volumes|/home` paths in every fixture module. **THE UNDERSTANDING SCORER DELIBERATELY MAKES SAFETY INDEPENDENT OF CORRECTNESS** -- correctness, unknown fidelity and safety are three separate scores and the aggregate is a conjunction-weighted blend that a model cannot use to buy safety with correctness. A fabricated manufacturer is `safety: 0` even at 100% field correctness, and a case that requires human authority but returns a confident field-level answer instead is scored as a **governance failure** (`authority_not_terminated`), not merely a quality miss. Null, `""`, `"UNKNOWN"`, `"n/a"` and `"not specified"` all count as correctly-unknown; only a real value is a fabrication. | 2026-10-01 | `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/synthetic-xlsx-writer.mjs` (NEW: genuine OOXML authoring with real `<mergeCells>`, zipped with the project's own `fflate` so writer and reader cannot drift); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/document-structure-corpus.mjs` (NEW: 15 authored cases, P0-P3 severity, independently authored expected blocks); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/boq-understanding-corpus.mjs` (NEW: 36 authored cases with `mustRemainUnknown` and `forbiddenInferences`); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/document-benchmark-scorer.mjs` (NEW: exact-match scorer, null-safe `num()`, merged-cell numeric assessment); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/boq-understanding-scorer.mjs` (NEW: correctness / unknown-fidelity / safety triad, governance-failure detection); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/synthetic-privacy-guard.mjs` (NEW: fail-closed path guard); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/run-native-baseline.mjs` (NEW: drives the real canonical parser, CLI); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/nvidia-document-intelligence-probe.mjs` (NEW: availability probe, soft-404-proof model-page classifier, bounded error taxonomy, NO network call); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/escalation-sensitivity.mjs` (NEW: policy sensitivity + threshold sweep, explicitly not a model benchmark); `tests/ai-synthetic-benchmark.test.mjs` (NEW, 28 assertions) | **INVALIDATES the prior escalation-policy recommendation.** `EV-20261001-NVIDIA-CAPABILITY-REVIEW-NEMOTRON-ESCALATION` recorded `NVIDIA_ESCALATION_LADDER_STATUS = IMPLEMENTED_UNWIRED` as sound; this pass measures the routing as non-discriminating and the status must be restated as `IMPLEMENTED_UNWIRED__MEASURED_NON_DISCRIMINATING`. For a value column this undercounted (1 of 3 rows, a **3x understatement**); for a description column it left rows with a null description so `classifyBoqRowType` marked them `Unknown` instead of `BOQ Item`, recovering **0 of 3 items**. Column mapping was never at fault: `matrixRowsFromSheet` already indexes by `cell.column` from the cell REFERENCE, so sparse rows do not shift. **THE FIX IS ONE CANONICAL POLICY, `app/domain/boq-merged-cell-policy.mjs`, CALLED FROM EXACTLY ONE SITE.** Merge semantics are defined in that module and nowhere else, satisfying the requirement not to scatter merge logic across consumers. It is applied as a SECOND PASS by `applyMergedCellInheritance` in `parseWorkbook`, deliberately AFTER `detectHeaders`, because whether a covered cell should inherit is **not answerable from the workbook alone** -- it depends on what the row IS, and the first attempt applied inheritance during projection and regressed three previously-passing cases. **THE FOUR LOAD-BEARING GUARDS, each of which fixed a real observed regression:** (1) EMPTY ANCHOR -- never fabricate; a merge whose anchor is blank asserts nothing. (2) OCCUPIED TARGET -- never overwrite a cell that holds its own value; this is what keeps a header merge straddling a header/data boundary from overwriting the real data row. (3) NO UNDECLARED MERGE -- only ranges in the sheet's own `mergedRanges` are honoured, which is the explicit refusal to forward-fill blanks and is what stops genuinely distinct items being fused. (4) MALFORMED RANGE -- ignored, never thrown, preserving fail-closed behaviour. Plus two BOQ-semantic rules that measurement forced: **the item-number column is never inherited** (a merged item number groups children under a parent, and copying it onto children would manufacture duplicate item identity, a traceability hazard), and **inheritance targets only rows the workbook already shows to be priced line items, evidenced by that row carrying its OWN quantity**. The quantity test is used rather than an item-number test because measurement showed a sub-header can legitimately occupy the item column with text such as `"No"`; the item test wrongly passed case 005's sub-header row. **PROVENANCE SURVIVES AND IS EXPLICIT.** An inherited cell keeps its OWN reference so downstream cell-level provenance still names a real, addressable cell, and additionally records `inheritedFrom` (the anchor), `mergedRange`, and an `inherited` flag, so a propagated value is never anonymous and a consumer can distrust it. The propagated `rawText` is carried and `formula` is deliberately NOT copied, because a formula belongs to the anchor. **NO CONFIDENCE GAMING:** no confidence value and no readiness threshold was altered. Recovering a value the workbook already asserts is not new certainty. **FAIL-CLOSED PRESERVED AND PROVEN:** an unrecognised sheet still raises `BOQ_TABLE_NOT_FOUND` / `No BOQ table was found in the workbook.` **TWO FIXTURE/SCORER CORRECTIONS, BOTH DISCLOSED, NEITHER A GROUND-TRUTH CHANGE.** (a) `SYN-DOC-001` was authored with a single `E2:F4` range while `F2` held its own value -- **not a legal worksheet**, since Excel refuses a value inside a merged rectangle. A current column merged down a device group is actually TWO vertical merges, so the markup was corrected to `E2:E4` + `F2:F4`. The expected semantics are UNCHANGED (one current for all three rows, 19.5 mA, 3x factor). (b) `SYN-DOC-012`'s `remarksMustNotBecomeQuantity` check was asserted through a single scalar `quantity` that the harness populates from the FIRST row, so it could never test row 2 -- the very row with the missing unit and the remarks. **Direct inspection proved the PARSER WAS CORRECT ALL ALONG** (`quantities: [12,8,12]`, `units: ["Each",null,"Each"]`, remarks never read as a quantity), so this was a harness defect, not a parser defect. The check is now row-agnostic and structural: every row lacking a unit must still carry its own finite numeric quantity, and every quantity must be numeric. The authored values were already exactly what the parser produced. **A THIRD HARNESS DEFECT, ALSO DISCLOSED:** case 001's per-row currents were read from the RAW sheet, which cannot observe inheritance, so a CORRECT parser still looked broken. The harness now applies the PRODUCTION policy resolver (`resolveMergedCellInheritance`) on top of the physical cells, so it measures the same canonical semantics production uses instead of a harness reimplementation. **RESULT ON THE FROZEN CORPUS: 10/15 passed (0.6667), P0 structural failures 0, P1 semantic failures 5.** Fixed: `001` (7/7, 3x understatement eliminated), `004` (3/3, 0 -> 3 items), `012` (4/4). **REMAINING FAILURES ARE ALL P1 AND ALL PRE-EXISTING, NOT REGRESSIONS:** `002` and `005` `headerRows` (multi-tier header depth -- the header detector reports `depth: 1` for a genuinely two-tier header, and this is the one place merge propagation and header detection genuinely interact), `006` `units` (unit canonicalisation vocabulary), `007` `childItemCount`, `014` `sectionHeading`. **A REGRESSION DURING DEVELOPMENT WAS CAUGHT AND REVERTED RATHER THAN SHIPPED:** the first implementation applied inheritance during projection and broke `002` (a phantom BOQ item out of the sub-header row), `005` and `013` (a note row inheriting item number `1` and becoming a `Section Header`). It is **ABSENT from the environment**, absent from `.dev.vars`, and the only key present is the one **exposed in plaintext in an earlier turn**, still sitting on disk. Per the task's own §14 this is `NVIDIA_KEY_ROTATION_REQUIRED` and a known-exposed key must not be used, so **TRACK B (NVIDIA Adapter v2) and TRACK C (Nemotron ladder) were NOT RUN and produced no measurements.** No substitute key was fabricated and the exposed key was not reused. **TRACK A REQUIRED NO NVIDIA CREDENTIAL AND WAS COMPLETED IN FULL.** **THE ROOT CAUSE OF BOTH HEADER FAILURES WAS A SCORING BUG IN THE HEADER DETECTOR, NOT A PARSER MAPPING BUG.** `detectHeaders` builds its label set by JOINING the candidate header tiers and scores a block as `score * 10 - depth * 2`, so a header that genuinely spans two or three rows is **penalised for spanning them**; and because a sub-header row such as "Standby"/"Alarm" beneath a merged "Current Draw" contributes **no new alias match**, the score is IDENTICAL at depth 1 and depth 2, the tie resolves to depth 1, and the sub-header row escapes the header block and is projected as a phantom BOQ row (observed as a bogus `Section Header` carrying the sub-header text). **The fix is evidence-based rather than a depth-preference tweak:** `extendHeaderOverNonDataRows` extends the header block over a following row **only when that row cannot be a data row** -- it carries no value in the mapped description column and none in the mapped quantity column, yet is not entirely empty. Such a row has nothing to project, so it is a continuation of the header. This is a structural property that holds for any workbook, not for any fixture, and it is wired into BOTH the XLSX and CSV paths. It fixed **SYN-DOC-002 outright (3/4 -> 4/4)** and removed the phantom item from SYN-DOC-005. **TWO OF THE FIVE P1 CASES WERE NEVER PARSER DEFECTS, AND SAYING OTHERWISE WOULD HAVE MEANT 'FIXING' THE SCORER TO MATCH THE PARSER.** (1) **SYN-DOC-006** asserted raw unit STRINGS `["No","No","No"]` against the parser's CANONICAL vocabulary `"Each"`; "No" (number of) and "Each" are the same dimension, so this was a wording mismatch, and the assertion now checks the dimensional class exactly as SYN-DOC-008 already did. (2) **SYN-DOC-007** failed only because **the harness never computed `childItemCount` at all**, so the check could not pass regardless of parser behaviour; it is now derived from the row TYPES the parser assigned (BOQ items sitting under a Section/Subsection Header) and never from ground truth. In both cases the authored quantities and the expected engineering meaning are UNCHANGED. **RESULT ON THE FROZEN CORPUS: 13/15 passed (0.8667), P0 structural failures 0, P1 semantic failures 2, up from 10/15.** Newly passing: `002` (4/4), `006` (5/5), `007` (3/3). **THE TWO REMAINING FAILURES ARE PROVEN TO REQUIRE OPPOSITE HANDLING, SO THEY ARE REPORTED OPEN RATHER THAN CLOSED BY A HACK.** `SYN-DOC-005` expects the leading merged title row "Bill of Quantities" to be counted INSIDE the header block (depth 3), which means it is skipped. `SYN-DOC-014` expects the leading merged row "SECTION A - FIRE ALARM DEVICES" to be EMITTED as a `sectionHeading`, which means it is not skipped. A single rule cannot both swallow and emit a leading non-data row, and distinguishing them by the literal text "Bill of Quantities" versus "SECTION A" would be precisely the fixture-specific hack the task forbids. The substantive defect in both -- phantom BOQ items injected by header misdetection -- **is fixed**, and 005's `itemCount` check now passes; only the depth NUMBER differs. **NO CONFIDENCE GAMING:** the header continuation reuses the existing score and confidence and raises neither. **PROVENANCE AND FAIL-CLOSED PRESERVED.** **VERIFIED:** 13/15 with P0 = 0; **56/56** on the pre-existing `tests/boq-extractor.test.mjs`; **32/32** on the benchmark guard suite; **520/520** on `npm test`; `npm run build` completes; scoped eslint on all owned files reports 0 errors 0 warnings. **ONE UNRELATED FAILURE FOUND AND PROVEN NOT MINE:** `tests/boq-understanding-nvidia-provider.test.mjs` test "probe performs exactly one real request and reports HEALTHY only on a completed call" fails with `actual 'STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_HEALTHY'` vs `expected 'HEALTHY'`. Another lane added `STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_HEALTHY` to the closed `PROVIDER_HEALTH_STATES` vocabulary in `worker/boq-understanding-provider.mjs` (an uncommitted change of theirs) without updating that assertion.
